# Implementation Plan: Sprint 3 — `models/ltx2/` Resync + Cameraman/Dedup Tests (Revision 3)

## Overview
Execute Sprint 3 of the Wan2GP fork migration (`docs/WAN2GP_FORK_MIGRATION_PLAN.md:125-161`). Three work streams:

1. **Submodule hot-zone resync** — advance `Wan2GP/` (submodule whose `.gitmodules` names `reigh-sprint-2` but sits in a detached-HEAD checkout) to a pinned SHA on `banodoco/Wan2GP reigh-sprint-3`. Scope: bucket-`(a)` triage rows under `Wan2GP/models/ltx2/` **and** `Wan2GP/defaults/ltx2_22B_distilled_1_1.json`; `clear_conditioning` at `Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx_core/tools.py:66-79` stays byte-identical.
2. **Defaults addition** — land `ltx2_22B_distilled_1_1.json` as discoverable-but-not-default per SD-004.
3. **First-class test deliverables** — `tests/test_travel_guidance_ic_lora_override.py` + `tests/test_task_registry_ic_lora_dedup.py` to pin the currently-uncovered branches at `source/core/params/travel_guidance.py:305-325` and `source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py:955-971`.

**Sandbox constraints (precise):**
- Executor **cannot write `.git/`** (index, refs, hooks, packed-refs, objects). That forbids `git add`, `git commit`, `git tag`, `git push`, submodule pointer bumps, and `.gitmodules` edits (the file itself is a working-tree text file the executor *can* read; Sprint 2 convention is the orchestrator owns the edit in concert with the submodule bump). All such ops are handed off via `megaplan override add-note`.
- Executor **cannot reach external hosts** (sandbox DNS blocks github.com). Any gate that needs `git ls-remote` or network fetch is delegated to the orchestrator. Local-only checks stay with the executor.
- `uv sync` fails on darwin-arm64 (decord==0.6.0). Build `/tmp/sprint3-smoke-venv` on-demand with the Sprint 2 set: `gradio torch diffusers transformers mmgp scipy Pillow numpy torchvision opencv-python imageio rembg onnxruntime ffmpeg-python`. Pure-pytest gates use `uv run --python 3.10 pytest …`.
- Executor leaves working-tree edits unstaged; produces a file list + patch + tarball bundle that the orchestrator uses to `git add`.

**Current baseline (re-probed):**
- `.gitmodules` names branch `reigh-sprint-2`; `Wan2GP/` is detached HEAD → `git -C Wan2GP rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD` returns `HEAD`. SHA-based validation only.
- `clear_conditioning` at `Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx_core/tools.py:66-79`, 14 lines.
- `_IC_LORA_BY_MODE`/`_IC_LORA_UNION_CONTROL` at `source/core/params/travel_guidance.py:29-37`; `get_ic_lora_entry()` at 305-325.
- Dedup inlined at `source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py:962-970`.
- `ltx2_22B_distilled_1_1.json` does not exist under `Wan2GP/defaults/` today.
- Sole caller of `get_ic_lora_entry()` is `source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py:958`.
- `clear_conditioning` live callers: `Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py:1257,1259,1276`.
- `source/models/wgp/orchestrator.py` documents that `wgp.py` reads `defaults/*.json` **relative to cwd** → any discovery probe must `chdir('Wan2GP')` before import.

### Step 4: Extract dedup helper + write `tests/test_task_registry_ic_lora_dedup.py`
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Refactor** `source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py:961-970` → module-level helper:
   ```python
   def _inject_ic_lora_with_dedup(segment_loras, ic_entry):
       segment_loras = list(segment_loras or [])
       ic_basename = os.path.basename(ic_entry["path"])
       for existing in segment_loras:
           if os.path.basename(existing.get("path", "")) == ic_basename:
               existing["strength"] = ic_entry["strength"]
               return segment_loras
       segment_loras.append(ic_entry)
       return segment_loras
   ```
   Replace inline block with one call; keep `headless_logger.debug(...)`. Zero behavioral change.
2. **Create** `tests/test_task_registry_ic_lora_dedup.py` importing `from source.task_handlers.tasks.task_registry import _inject_ic_lora_with_dedup`.
3. **`test_dedup_updates_strength_when_basename_matches`**: seed `[{"path": "https://example.com/ltx-2.3-22b-ic-lora-union-control-ref0.5.safetensors", "strength": 0.4}]`; inject `{"path": "ltx-2.3-22b-ic-lora-union-control-ref0.5.safetensors", "strength": 0.8, "name": "ic-lora-pose (auto-injected)"}`; assert `len == 1` and `strength == 0.8`.
4. **`test_dedup_appends_when_no_match`**: seed empty; inject any entry; assert `len == 1` and item equals injected entry.

## Known narrowings (accepted tradeoffs)

- **callers-1 / callers (IC-LoRA integration — truthful narrowing)**: The existing 6-file travel suite IC-LoRA tests (`tests/test_travel_ltx_vpt.py:101-132,255-284` and `tests/test_ltx_hybrid_travel.py:364-392`) exercise the legacy `_auto_inject_travel_guidance_lora` helper, **not** the current `source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py:_build_generation_params` path that wires `get_ic_lora_entry()` → `activated_loras`/`loras_multipliers`. Sprint 3 therefore does **not** add new integration coverage of that wiring; the new unit tests pin `get_ic_lora_entry()` value branches and the extracted `_inject_ic_lora_with_dedup` helper in isolation. Behavioral preservation of the downstream wiring is guaranteed only by the fact that Step 4's helper extraction is a pure syntactic no-op (same operations, same sequence, same debug log). Accepted: this is a known coverage gap — patching it would require a new integration fixture outside the Sprint 3 scope stated in the idea text.
- **callers-2 (`clear_conditioning` single-shape probe)**: Live callers at `Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py:1257,1259,1276` invoke `clear_conditioning` with video/audio/preview shapes, but `tools.py:66-79` is a uniform slice with no shape-dependent branches. One synthetic fixture exercises the full logic.

  "meta_commentary": "Sprint 3 of the Wan2GP fork migration. Four deliverables, eight plan steps, eleven tasks. The critical path is: (T1 handoff #1) \u2192 [executor-local work T3/T4/T5 in parallel is fine] \u2192 (T6 baseline gates) \u2192 (T7 orchestrator resync wait) \u2192 (T8 defaults verification) \u2192 (T9 post-resync gates) \u2192 (T10 handoff #2) \u2192 (T11 final sanity).\n\n**Sandbox boundaries (non-negotiable):** Executor cannot write .git/ (no git add/commit/tag/push) and cannot reach github.com (no ls-remote). Every .git/ write and every network-requiring git op is orchestrator-owned via `megaplan override add-note`. Violate either and the sprint breaks.\n\n**The two handoff notes are the operational spine:** #1 before T7 (tag + branch advance + submodule bump + remote-SHA record instructions), #2 after T9 (gate results + file list + LOCAL_SHA + orchestrator-side remote-SHA compare with block-on-mismatch). Do not skip either. Read the plan's Step 1.2 and Step 8.3 for exact content.\n\n**clear_conditioning byte-identity is the sprint's existential invariant.** If test_clear_conditioning_byte_identity.py fails post-resync, do NOT regenerate the oracle \u2014 that would silently accept the drift. Escalate to the orchestrator to fork-patch banodoco's version. The test is the proof; a failure blocks the whole sprint.\n\n**Dedup extraction is a pure no-op refactor.** There is no integration test for the downstream activated_loras/loras_multipliers wiring \u2014 behavioral preservation comes from the extraction being syntactically equivalent to the inlined block. Diff the refactor carefully against task_registry.py:961-970. The `headless_logger.debug(...)` call stays at its original caller site; the helper itself emits no log.\n\n**Two grep-trap landmines from the critique history:** (1) Step 6.5 non-default assertion must be TWO separate greps (source/ and Wan2GP/defaults/ minus self-reference), not one combined grep \u2014 the new JSON's content will always match its own name. (2) The Step 6 discovery probe MUST `os.chdir('Wan2GP')` before importing the wgp bridge, because wgp.py reads defaults/*.json relative to cwd (documented in source/models/wgp/orchestrator.py). Both are flagged in the plan's FLAG-S3-007 and correctness-3.\n\n**Anchor-test count (SD-S3-D, approver-settled):** Do NOT re-author tests/test_travel_guidance_config.py. '20 anchor tests' reads as \u226520 total collected + \u226512 anchor-named supplementary. This was flagged three iterations in a row and explicitly settled; iteration pressure is noise, not signal.\n\n**Handoff packaging (SD-S3-K):** Emit all three artifacts. `git diff HEAD` excludes untracked adds, so working_tree.patch alone will only carry the task_registry.py refactor \u2014 the tarball is how the 5 untracked files reach the orchestrator. (b) no integration test for get_ic_lora_entry \u2192 activated_loras wiring \u2014 legacy travel-suite tests exercise _auto_inject_travel_guidance_lora, not the current path. Neither is a blocker for this sprint.\n\n**Execution hygiene:** Use `uv run --python 3.10 pytest ...` for all pytest gates (bypasses the decord wheel gap).       "rationale": "The defaults JSON is an explicit Sprint 3 deliverable per the idea text. Scope widening ensures Step 6's discoverability check has a file to verify."

      "id": "SD-S3-F",
      "decision": "Strict path-contract smoke from `docs/WAN2GP_FORK_MIGRATION_PLAN.md:106-108` is rerun pre- and post-resync inside `/tmp/sprint3-smoke-venv` (built on-demand with the Sprint 2 package set).",
      "rationale": "Protected behavior per migration plan line 161 \u2014 dotted/bare-module `Wan2GP.*` reachability must survive the hot-zone resync."

      "decision": "Remote-SHA comparison for the `Wan2GP/` submodule pointer is executed by the orchestrator via `git ls-remote`, not the executor. Executor records `LOCAL_SHA` from `git -C Wan2GP rev-parse HEAD` and delegates the remote compare via handoff #2. `.gitmodules` branch entry check (`reigh-sprint-3`) stays executor-side (local file read only).",
      "rationale": "Sandbox DNS blocks github.com, making `git ls-remote` non-executable by the executor."
    },
    {
      "id": "SD-S3-H",
      "decision": "Defaults-discovery probe `os.chdir('Wan2GP')` before calling `get_model_def('ltx2_22B_distilled_1_1')` in the Step 6 `python -c` block. Filesystem-glob fallback (which does not require cwd awareness) is acceptable if `/tmp/sprint3-smoke-venv` is unavailable.",
      "rationale": "`source/models/wgp/orchestrator.py` documents that `wgp.py` reads `defaults/*.json` relative to cwd; calling without chdir can misreport discovery even for a present, valid JSON."

None write to `.git/`.",
      "rationale": "`git diff HEAD` excludes untracked adds; without the tarball, the patch would omit most of the sprint's deliverables. `git add -N` would update `.git/index` and violate the sandbox rule."

        Settled decisions (verify the executor implemented these correctly):
- SD-S3-A: Extract the inlined dedup at `source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py:961-970` into module-level `_inject_ic_lora_with_dedup(segment_loras, ic_entry)` as a pure no-op refactor. Tests import the helper directly. (Enables unit-level pinning of the two dedup branches without rebuilding the full `convert_to_wgp_task_impl` fixture surface. Behavioral preservation is guaranteed by the no-op nature of the extraction (same ops, same sequence, existing debug log preserved).)
- SD-S3-B: `clear_conditioning` byte-compare uses a single deterministic CPU-only offline synthetic `LatentState` fixture at `tests/fixtures/clear_conditioning_oracle.pt`, captured by `scripts/sprint3/capture_clear_conditioning_fixture.py` (not rerun by CI). (`tools.py:66-79` has no shape-dependent branches (uniform slice). One fixture exercises the full function logic. Broader caller-shape coverage is deferred as an accepted narrowing (callers-2).)
- SD-S3-C: All `.git/` writes and all external-network operations are orchestrator-owned. Executor never runs `git add`, `git commit`, `git tag`, `git push`, or `git ls-remote` against github.com. (Carry-forward of the Sprint 2 sandbox rule and the iteration-2 DNS-blocked-to-github.com finding. Remote-SHA compare is delegated to the orchestrator in handoff #2.)
- SD-S3-D: '20 anchor tests' (`docs/WAN2GP_FORK_MIGRATION_PLAN.md:146`) is read as '`tests/test_travel_guidance_config.py --collect-only` yields ≥20 total cases' (primary gate) + 'anchor-named case count ≥12' (supplementary check). Approver-settled; persistent re-flagging does not reopen. (The file currently collects ~39 parametrized cases, ~13 anchor-named. Re-authoring to exactly-20 anchor-named is outside the sprint's four-deliverable scope.)
- SD-S3-E: Sprint 3 resync scope on `banodoco/Wan2GP reigh-sprint-3` covers bucket-(a) rows under both `Wan2GP/models/ltx2/` and `Wan2GP/defaults/ltx2_22B_distilled_1_1.json`. Invariant: `clear_conditioning` at `Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx_core/tools.py:66-79` stays byte-identical. (The defaults JSON is an explicit Sprint 3 deliverable per the idea text. Scope widening ensures Step 6's discoverability check has a file to verify.)
- SD-S3-F: Strict path-contract smoke from `docs/WAN2GP_FORK_MIGRATION_PLAN.md:106-108` is rerun pre- and post-resync inside `/tmp/sprint3-smoke-venv` (built on-demand with the Sprint 2 package set). (Protected behavior per migration plan line 161 — dotted/bare-module `Wan2GP.*` reachability must survive the hot-zone resync.)
- SD-S3-G: Remote-SHA comparison for the `Wan2GP/` submodule pointer is executed by the orchestrator via `git ls-remote`, not the executor. Executor records `LOCAL_SHA` from `git -C Wan2GP rev-parse HEAD` and delegates the remote compare via handoff #2. `.gitmodules` branch entry check (`reigh-sprint-3`) stays executor-side (local file read only). (Sandbox DNS blocks github.com, making `git ls-remote` non-executable by the executor.)
- SD-S3-H: Defaults-discovery probe `os.chdir('Wan2GP')` before calling `get_model_def('ltx2_22B_distilled_1_1')` in the Step 6 `python -c` block. Filesystem-glob fallback (which does not require cwd awareness) is acceptable if `/tmp/sprint3-smoke-venv` is unavailable. (`source/models/wgp/orchestrator.py` documents that `wgp.py` reads `defaults/*.json` relative to cwd; calling without chdir can misreport discovery even for a present, valid JSON.)
- SD-S3-I: IC-LoRA integration wiring (`get_ic_lora_entry()` → `activated_loras`/`loras_multipliers` at `source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py:973-987`) is a known coverage gap for Sprint 3. The existing travel-suite IC-LoRA tests exercise the legacy `_auto_inject_travel_guidance_lora` helper, not the current path. Behavioral preservation of the dedup extraction relies on it being a pure syntactic no-op, not on test coverage. (Adding an integration fixture for the current path is outside the sprint's four-deliverable scope. The gap is a truthful narrowing, not a hidden assumption.)
- SD-S3-J: Non-default assertion splits into two explicit checks to prevent the new JSON's self-reference from producing false positives: (1) `grep -rln 'ltx2_22B_distilled_1_1' source/` returns zero, (2) `grep -rln 'ltx2_22B_distilled_1_1' Wan2GP/defaults/` excluding the new JSON file itself returns zero. (A single combined grep over `source/ Wan2GP/defaults/*.json` will always match the new JSON's own content and is not machine-checkable.)
- SD-S3-K: Step 8 handoff bundle uses three artifacts: `staged_file_list.txt` (path list), `working_tree.patch` (tracked-edit diff via `git diff HEAD -- <tracked_path>` captured via subprocess + `pathlib.Path.write_bytes()`), and `unstaged_additions.tar` (tarball of the five untracked new files via `tar -cf`). None write to `.git/`. (`git diff HEAD` excludes untracked adds; without the tarball, the patch would omit most of the sprint's deliverables. `git add -N` would update `.git/index` and violate the sandbox rule.)

        Requirements:
        - Verify each success criterion explicitly.
        - Trust executor evidence by default. Dig deeper only where the git diff, `execution_audit.json`, or vague notes make the claim ambiguous.
        - Each criterion has a `priority` (`must`, `should`, or `info`). Apply these rules:
          - `must` criteria are hard gates. A `must` criterion that fails means `needs_rework`.
          - `should` criteria are quality targets. If the spirit is met but the letter is not, mark `pass` with evidence explaining the gap. Only mark `fail` if the intent was clearly missed. A `should` failure alone does NOT require `needs_rework`.
          - `info` criteria are for human reference. Mark them `waived` with a note — do not evaluate them.
          - If a criterion has `requires` capabilities that are not satisfiable by container workers (e.g., `drive_browser`, `subjective_judgment`), mark it `deferred_human` — NOT `fail` or `waived`. Deferred-human criteria do NOT count toward `needs_rework`.
          - If a criterion (any priority) cannot be verified in this context (e.g., requires manual testing or runtime observation), mark it `waived` with an explanation.
        - Set `review_verdict` to `needs_rework` only when at least one `must` criterion fails or actual implementation work is incomplete. Use `approved` when all `must` criteria pass, even if some `should` criteria are flagged.
        - The decisions listed above were settled at the gate stage. Verify that the executor implemented each settled decision correctly. Flag deviations from these decisions, but do not question the decisions themselves.
        - baseline_test_failures in finalize.json lists tests that were already failing before execution. Do not flag these as rework items unless the executor introduced new failures in those same tests.
        - Cross-reference each task's `files_changed` and `commands_run` against the git diff and any audit findings.
        - Review every `sense_check` explicitly and treat perfunctory acknowledgments as a reason to dig deeper.
        - Follow this JSON shape exactly:
        ```json
        {
          "review_verdict": "approved",
          "criteria": [
            {
              "name": "All existing tests pass",
              "priority": "must",
              "pass": "pass",
              "evidence": "Test suite ran green — 42 passed, 0 failed."
            },
            {
              "name": "File under ~300 lines",
              "priority": "should",
              "pass": "pass",
              "evidence": "File is 375 lines — above the target but reasonable given the component's responsibilities. Spirit met."
            },
            {
              "name": "Manual smoke tests pass",
              "priority": "info",
              "pass": "waived",
              "evidence": "Cannot be verified in automated review. Noted for manual QA."
            }
          ],
          "issues": [],
          "rework_items": [],
          "summary": "Approved. All must criteria pass. The should criterion on line count is close enough given the component scope.",
          "task_verdicts": [
            {
              "task_id": "T6",
              "reviewer_verdict": "Pass. Claimed handler changes and command evidence match the repo state.",
              "evidence_files": ["megaplan/handlers.py", "megaplan/evaluation.py"]
            }
          ],
          "sense_check_verdicts": [
            {
              "sense_check_id": "SC6",
              "verdict": "Confirmed. The execute blocker only fires when both evidence arrays are empty."
            }
          ]
        }
        ```
        - `rework_items` must be an array of structured rework directives. When `review_verdict` is `needs_rework`, populate one entry per issue with:
          - `task_id`: which finalize task this issue relates to
          - `issue`: what is wrong
          - `expected`: what correct behavior looks like
          - `actual`: what was observed
          - `evidence_file` (optional): file path supporting the finding
          - `flag_id`: critique/review flag ID when applicable, otherwise `null`
          - `source`: short machine-readable source tag when applicable, otherwise `null`
        - `issues` must still be populated as a flat one-line-per-item summary derived from `rework_items` (for backward compatibility). When approved, both `issues` and `rework_items` should be empty arrays.
        - When the work needs another execute pass, keep the same shape and change only `review_verdict` to `needs_rework`; make `issues`, `rework_items`, `summary`, and task verdicts specific enough for the executor to act on directly.

# Implementation Plan: Sprint 3 — `models/ltx2/` Resync + Cameraman/Dedup Tests (Revision 3)

## Overview
Execute Sprint 3 of the Wan2GP fork migration (`docs/WAN2GP_FORK_MIGRATION_PLAN.md:125-161`). Three work streams:

1. **Submodule hot-zone resync** — advance `Wan2GP/` (submodule whose `.gitmodules` names `reigh-sprint-2` but sits in a detached-HEAD checkout) to a pinned SHA on `banodoco/Wan2GP reigh-sprint-3`. Scope: bucket-`(a)` triage rows under `Wan2GP/models/ltx2/` **and** `Wan2GP/defaults/ltx2_22B_distilled_1_1.json`; `clear_conditioning` at `Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx_core/tools.py:66-79` stays byte-identical.
2. **Defaults addition** — land `ltx2_22B_distilled_1_1.json` as discoverable-but-not-default per SD-004.
3. **First-class test deliverables** — `tests/test_travel_guidance_ic_lora_override.py` + `tests/test_task_registry_ic_lora_dedup.py` to pin the currently-uncovered branches at `source/core/params/travel_guidance.py:305-325` and `source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py:955-971`.

**Sandbox constraints (precise):**
- Executor **cannot write `.git/`** (index, refs, hooks, packed-refs, objects). That forbids `git add`, `git commit`, `git tag`, `git push`, submodule pointer bumps, and `.gitmodules` edits (the file itself is a working-tree text file the executor *can* read; Sprint 2 convention is the orchestrator owns the edit in concert with the submodule bump). All such ops are handed off via `megaplan override add-note`.
- Executor **cannot reach external hosts** (sandbox DNS blocks github.com). Any gate that needs `git ls-remote` or network fetch is delegated to the orchestrator. Local-only checks stay with the executor.
- `uv sync` fails on darwin-arm64 (decord==0.6.0). Build `/tmp/sprint3-smoke-venv` on-demand with the Sprint 2 set: `gradio torch diffusers transformers mmgp scipy Pillow numpy torchvision opencv-python imageio rembg onnxruntime ffmpeg-python`. Pure-pytest gates use `uv run --python 3.10 pytest …`.
- Executor leaves working-tree edits unstaged; produces a file list + patch + tarball bundle that the orchestrator uses to `git add`.

**Current baseline (re-probed):**
- `.gitmodules` names branch `reigh-sprint-2`; `Wan2GP/` is detached HEAD → `git -C Wan2GP rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD` returns `HEAD`. SHA-based validation only.
- `clear_conditioning` at `Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx_core/tools.py:66-79`, 14 lines.
- `_IC_LORA_BY_MODE`/`_IC_LORA_UNION_CONTROL` at `source/core/params/travel_guidance.py:29-37`; `get_ic_lora_entry()` at 305-325.
- Dedup inlined at `source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py:962-970`.
- `ltx2_22B_distilled_1_1.json` does not exist under `Wan2GP/defaults/` today.
- Sole caller of `get_ic_lora_entry()` is `source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py:958`.
- `clear_conditioning` live callers: `Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py:1257,1259,1276`.
- `source/models/wgp/orchestrator.py` documents that `wgp.py` reads `defaults/*.json` **relative to cwd** → any discovery probe must `chdir('Wan2GP')` before import.

## Main Phase

Write both to `scripts/sprint3/_pre_resync_notes.txt` (working-tree scratch, unstaged). This is the `PRE_RESYNC_SHA` and pre-resync `clear_conditioning` reference.

   - Create tag `wan2gp-mig-sprint-3-baseline` on `reigh-worker` at current HEAD **before** any submodule pointer bump.

Invariant: bytes of `clear_conditioning` at `tools.py:66-79` must equal the pre-resync reference.

Build a synthetic `LatentState` and a stub whose `patchifier.get_token_count()` returns a fixed `num_tokens` smaller than the token dim. Call `clear_conditioning` bound to the stub. `torch.save({"inputs": {...}, "outputs": {...}}, "tests/fixtures/clear_conditioning_oracle.pt")`.

Known narrowing: single synthetic shape, accepted per SD-S3-B (see Known Narrowings).

### Step 4: Extract dedup helper + write `tests/test_task_registry_ic_lora_dedup.py`
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Refactor** `source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py:961-970` → module-level helper:
   ```python
   def _inject_ic_lora_with_dedup(segment_loras, ic_entry):
       segment_loras = list(segment_loras or [])
       ic_basename = os.path.basename(ic_entry["path"])
       for existing in segment_loras:
           if os.path.basename(existing.get("path", "")) == ic_basename:
               existing["strength"] = ic_entry["strength"]
               return segment_loras
       segment_loras.append(ic_entry)
       return segment_loras
   ```
   Replace inline block with one call; keep `headless_logger.debug(...)`. Zero behavioral change.
2. **Create** `tests/test_task_registry_ic_lora_dedup.py` importing `from source.task_handlers.tasks.task_registry import _inject_ic_lora_with_dedup`.
3. **`test_dedup_updates_strength_when_basename_matches`**: seed `[{"path": "https://example.com/ltx-2.3-22b-ic-lora-union-control-ref0.5.safetensors", "strength": 0.4}]`; inject `{"path": "ltx-2.3-22b-ic-lora-union-control-ref0.5.safetensors", "strength": 0.8, "name": "ic-lora-pose (auto-injected)"}`; assert `len == 1` and `strength == 0.8`.
4. **`test_dedup_appends_when_no_match`**: seed empty; inject any entry; assert `len == 1` and item equals injected entry.
5. **Integration coverage**: see Known Narrowings (callers-1) below — the downstream `activated_loras`/`loras_multipliers` wiring is **not** currently pinned by any test; the extraction is a pure no-op refactor so behavior is preserved by construction, not by test.

   test "$HITS_DEFAULTS" -eq 0 || { echo "FAIL: unexpected references in Wan2GP/defaults/"; exit 1; }

## Known narrowings (accepted tradeoffs)

- **callers-1 / callers (IC-LoRA integration — truthful narrowing)**: The existing 6-file travel suite IC-LoRA tests (`tests/test_travel_ltx_vpt.py:101-132,255-284` and `tests/test_ltx_hybrid_travel.py:364-392`) exercise the legacy `_auto_inject_travel_guidance_lora` helper, **not** the current `source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py:_build_generation_params` path that wires `get_ic_lora_entry()` → `activated_loras`/`loras_multipliers`. Sprint 3 therefore does **not** add new integration coverage of that wiring; the new unit tests pin `get_ic_lora_entry()` value branches and the extracted `_inject_ic_lora_with_dedup` helper in isolation. Behavioral preservation of the downstream wiring is guaranteed only by the fact that Step 4's helper extraction is a pure syntactic no-op (same operations, same sequence, same debug log). Accepted: this is a known coverage gap — patching it would require a new integration fixture outside the Sprint 3 scope stated in the idea text.
- **callers-2 (`clear_conditioning` single-shape probe)**: Live callers at `Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py:1257,1259,1276` invoke `clear_conditioning` with video/audio/preview shapes, but `tools.py:66-79` is a uniform slice with no shape-dependent branches. One synthetic fixture exercises the full logic.

## Execution Order
1. Steps 1–4 (executor-only): state capture, fixture, new tests, dedup helper extraction.
2. Step 5 (orchestrator-gated): submodule resync with widened scope.
3. Step 6 (executor-side verification of defaults JSON; cwd-corrected).
4. Step 7 (all verification gates; remote-SHA compare delegated to orchestrator).
5. Step 8 (executor produces file list + patch + tarball; orchestrator stages and tags).

## Validation Order
1. Pre-resync: Steps 2–4 tests pass against current code.
2. Pre-resync: Full 6-file travel suite green.
3. Pre-resync: Strict path-contract smoke green (baseline).
4. Orchestrator advances submodule (Step 5).
5. Post-resync: `tests/test_clear_conditioning_byte_identity.py` still green.
6. Post-resync: Full 6-file travel suite still green.
7. Post-resync: New IC-LoRA + dedup tests still green.
8. Post-resync: Strict path-contract smoke still green.
9. Post-resync: Local submodule checks (Step 7.7 — SHA captured, `.gitmodules` names `reigh-sprint-3`, optional fetch containment).
10. Post-resync: Defaults JSON discoverable via cwd-correct probe (Step 6).
11. Anchor count ≥ 20 total; anchor-specific ≥ 12.
12. `-k` grep returns zero lines.
13. Handoff #2 emitted with all gate results + SHAs; orchestrator runs `git ls-remote` and asserts SHA match as the last gate.

  "meta_commentary": "Sprint 3 of the Wan2GP fork migration. Four deliverables, eight plan steps, eleven tasks. The critical path is: (T1 handoff #1) \u2192 [executor-local work T3/T4/T5 in parallel is fine] \u2192 (T6 baseline gates) \u2192 (T7 orchestrator resync wait) \u2192 (T8 defaults verification) \u2192 (T9 post-resync gates) \u2192 (T10 handoff #2) \u2192 (T11 final sanity).\n\n**Sandbox boundaries (non-negotiable):** Executor cannot write .git/ (no git add/commit/tag/push) and cannot reach github.com (no ls-remote). Every .git/ write and every network-requiring git op is orchestrator-owned via `megaplan override add-note`. Violate either and the sprint breaks.\n\n**The two handoff notes are the operational spine:** #1 before T7 (tag + branch advance + submodule bump + remote-SHA record instructions), #2 after T9 (gate results + file list + LOCAL_SHA + orchestrator-side remote-SHA compare with block-on-mismatch). Do not skip either. Read the plan's Step 1.2 and Step 8.3 for exact content.\n\n**clear_conditioning byte-identity is the sprint's existential invariant.** If test_clear_conditioning_byte_identity.py fails post-resync, do NOT regenerate the oracle \u2014 that would silently accept the drift. Escalate to the orchestrator to fork-patch banodoco's version. The test is the proof; a failure blocks the whole sprint.\n\n**Dedup extraction is a pure no-op refactor.** There is no integration test for the downstream activated_loras/loras_multipliers wiring \u2014 behavioral preservation comes from the extraction being syntactically equivalent to the inlined block. Diff the refactor carefully against task_registry.py:961-970. The `headless_logger.debug(...)` call stays at its original caller site; the helper itself emits no log.\n\n**Two grep-trap landmines from the critique history:** (1) Step 6.5 non-default assertion must be TWO separate greps (source/ and Wan2GP/defaults/ minus self-reference), not one combined grep \u2014 the new JSON's content will always match its own name. (2) The Step 6 discovery probe MUST `os.chdir('Wan2GP')` before importing the wgp bridge, because wgp.py reads defaults/*.json relative to cwd (documented in source/models/wgp/orchestrator.py). Both are flagged in the plan's FLAG-S3-007 and correctness-3.\n\n**Anchor-test count (SD-S3-D, approver-settled):** Do NOT re-author tests/test_travel_guidance_config.py. '20 anchor tests' reads as \u226520 total collected + \u226512 anchor-named supplementary. This was flagged three iterations in a row and explicitly settled; iteration pressure is noise, not signal.\n\n**Handoff packaging (SD-S3-K):** Emit all three artifacts. `git diff HEAD` excludes untracked adds, so working_tree.patch alone will only carry the task_registry.py refactor \u2014 the tarball is how the 5 untracked files reach the orchestrator. Use subprocess + pathlib.Path.write_bytes for the patch (not shell redirection, which can prompt for .git/ access).\n\n**Known gaps (documented, accepted):** (a) single-shape clear_conditioning fixture \u2014 tools.py:66-79 has no shape-dependent branches, single fixture is sufficient. (b) no integration test for get_ic_lora_entry \u2192 activated_loras wiring \u2014 legacy travel-suite tests exercise _auto_inject_travel_guidance_lora, not the current path. Neither is a blocker for this sprint.\n\n**Execution hygiene:** Use `uv run --python 3.10 pytest ...` for all pytest gates (bypasses the decord wheel gap). Build /tmp/sprint3-smoke-venv on demand only for the discovery probe and path-contract smoke. If the venv build fails, the glob fallback is acceptable for the defaults check.",

      "rationale": "Sandbox DNS blocks github.com, making `git ls-remote` non-executable by the executor."

      "rationale": "`source/models/wgp/orchestrator.py` documents that `wgp.py` reads `defaults/*.json` relative to cwd; calling without chdir can misreport discovery even for a present, valid JSON."

      "decision": "Step 8 handoff bundle uses three artifacts: `staged_file_list.txt` (path list), `working_tree.patch` (tracked-edit diff via `git diff HEAD -- <tracked_path>` captured via subprocess + `pathlib.Path.write_bytes()`), and `unstaged_additions.tar` (tarball of the five untracked new files via `tar -cf`). None write to `.git/`.",
      "rationale": "`git diff HEAD` excludes untracked adds; without the tarball, the patch would omit most of the sprint's deliverables. `git add -N` would update `.git/index` and violate the sandbox rule."

        Settled decisions (verify the executor implemented these correctly):
- SD-S3-A: Extract the inlined dedup at `source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py:961-970` into module-level `_inject_ic_lora_with_dedup(segment_loras, ic_entry)` as a pure no-op refactor. Tests import the helper directly. (Enables unit-level pinning of the two dedup branches without rebuilding the full `convert_to_wgp_task_impl` fixture surface. Behavioral preservation is guaranteed by the no-op nature of the extraction (same ops, same sequence, existing debug log preserved).)
- SD-S3-B: `clear_conditioning` byte-compare uses a single deterministic CPU-only offline synthetic `LatentState` fixture at `tests/fixtures/clear_conditioning_oracle.pt`, captured by `scripts/sprint3/capture_clear_conditioning_fixture.py` (not rerun by CI). (`tools.py:66-79` has no shape-dependent branches (uniform slice). One fixture exercises the full function logic. Broader caller-shape coverage is deferred as an accepted narrowing (callers-2).)
- SD-S3-C: All `.git/` writes and all external-network operations are orchestrator-owned. Executor never runs `git add`, `git commit`, `git tag`, `git push`, or `git ls-remote` against github.com. (Carry-forward of the Sprint 2 sandbox rule and the iteration-2 DNS-blocked-to-github.com finding. Remote-SHA compare is delegated to the orchestrator in handoff #2.)
- SD-S3-D: '20 anchor tests' (`docs/WAN2GP_FORK_MIGRATION_PLAN.md:146`) is read as '`tests/test_travel_guidance_config.py --collect-only` yields ≥20 total cases' (primary gate) + 'anchor-named case count ≥12' (supplementary check). Approver-settled; persistent re-flagging does not reopen. (The file currently collects ~39 parametrized cases, ~13 anchor-named. Re-authoring to exactly-20 anchor-named is outside the sprint's four-deliverable scope.)
- SD-S3-E: Sprint 3 resync scope on `banodoco/Wan2GP reigh-sprint-3` covers bucket-(a) rows under both `Wan2GP/models/ltx2/` and `Wan2GP/defaults/ltx2_22B_distilled_1_1.json`. Invariant: `clear_conditioning` at `Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx_core/tools.py:66-79` stays byte-identical. (The defaults JSON is an explicit Sprint 3 deliverable per the idea text. Scope widening ensures Step 6's discoverability check has a file to verify.)
- SD-S3-F: Strict path-contract smoke from `docs/WAN2GP_FORK_MIGRATION_PLAN.md:106-108` is rerun pre- and post-resync inside `/tmp/sprint3-smoke-venv` (built on-demand with the Sprint 2 package set). (Protected behavior per migration plan line 161 — dotted/bare-module `Wan2GP.*` reachability must survive the hot-zone resync.)
- SD-S3-G: Remote-SHA comparison for the `Wan2GP/` submodule pointer is executed by the orchestrator via `git ls-remote`, not the executor. Executor records `LOCAL_SHA` from `git -C Wan2GP rev-parse HEAD` and delegates the remote compare via handoff #2. `.gitmodules` branch entry check (`reigh-sprint-3`) stays executor-side (local file read only). (Sandbox DNS blocks github.com, making `git ls-remote` non-executable by the executor.)
- SD-S3-H: Defaults-discovery probe `os.chdir('Wan2GP')` before calling `get_model_def('ltx2_22B_distilled_1_1')` in the Step 6 `python -c` block. Filesystem-glob fallback (which does not require cwd awareness) is acceptable if `/tmp/sprint3-smoke-venv` is unavailable. (`source/models/wgp/orchestrator.py` documents that `wgp.py` reads `defaults/*.json` relative to cwd; calling without chdir can misreport discovery even for a present, valid JSON.)
- SD-S3-I: IC-LoRA integration wiring (`get_ic_lora_entry()` → `activated_loras`/`loras_multipliers` at `source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py:973-987`) is a known coverage gap for Sprint 3. The existing travel-suite IC-LoRA tests exercise the legacy `_auto_inject_travel_guidance_lora` helper, not the current path. Behavioral preservation of the dedup extraction relies on it being a pure syntactic no-op, not on test coverage. (Adding an integration fixture for the current path is outside the sprint's four-deliverable scope. The gap is a truthful narrowing, not a hidden assumption.)
- SD-S3-J: Non-default assertion splits into two explicit checks to prevent the new JSON's self-reference from producing false positives: (1) `grep -rln 'ltx2_22B_distilled_1_1' source/` returns zero, (2) `grep -rln 'ltx2_22B_distilled_1_1' Wan2GP/defaults/` excluding the new JSON file itself returns zero. (A single combined grep over `source/ Wan2GP/defaults/*.json` will always match the new JSON's own content and is not machine-checkable.)
- SD-S3-K: Step 8 handoff bundle uses three artifacts: `staged_file_list.txt` (path list), `working_tree.patch` (tracked-edit diff via `git diff HEAD -- <tracked_path>` captured via subprocess + `pathlib.Path.write_bytes()`), and `unstaged_additions.tar` (tarball of the five untracked new files via `tar -cf`). None write to `.git/`. (`git diff HEAD` excludes untracked adds; without the tarball, the patch would omit most of the sprint's deliverables. `git add -N` would update `.git/index` and violate the sandbox rule.)

        Requirements:
        - Verify each success criterion explicitly.
        - Trust executor evidence by default. Dig deeper only where the git diff, `execution_audit.json`, or vague notes make the claim ambiguous.
        - Each criterion has a `priority` (`must`, `should`, or `info`). Apply these rules:
          - `must` criteria are hard gates. A `must` criterion that fails means `needs_rework`.
          - `should` criteria are quality targets. If the spirit is met but the letter is not, mark `pass` with evidence explaining the gap. Only mark `fail` if the intent was clearly missed. A `should` failure alone does NOT require `needs_rework`.
          - `info` criteria are for human reference. Mark them `waived` with a note — do not evaluate them.
          - If a criterion has `requires` capabilities that are not satisfiable by container workers (e.g., `drive_browser`, `subjective_judgment`), mark it `deferred_human` — NOT `fail` or `waived`. Deferred-human criteria do NOT count toward `needs_rework`.
          - If a criterion (any priority) cannot be verified in this context (e.g., requires manual testing or runtime observation), mark it `waived` with an explanation.
        - Set `review_verdict` to `needs_rework` only when at least one `must` criterion fails or actual implementation work is incomplete. Use `approved` when all `must` criteria pass, even if some `should` criteria are flagged.
        - The decisions listed above were settled at the gate stage. Verify that the executor implemented each settled decision correctly. Flag deviations from these decisions, but do not question the decisions themselves.
        - baseline_test_failures in finalize.json lists tests that were already failing before execution. Do not flag these as rework items unless the executor introduced new failures in those same tests.
        - Cross-reference each task's `files_changed` and `commands_run` against the git diff and any audit findings.
        - Review every `sense_check` explicitly and treat perfunctory acknowledgments as a reason to dig deeper.
        - Follow this JSON shape exactly:
        ```json
        {
          "review_verdict": "approved",
          "criteria": [
            {
              "name": "All existing tests pass",
              "priority": "must",
              "pass": "pass",
              "evidence": "Test suite ran green — 42 passed, 0 failed."
            },
            {
              "name": "File under ~300 lines",
              "priority": "should",
              "pass": "pass",
              "evidence": "File is 375 lines — above the target but reasonable given the component's responsibilities. Spirit met."
            },
            {
              "name": "Manual smoke tests pass",
              "priority": "info",
              "pass": "waived",
              "evidence": "Cannot be verified in automated review. Noted for manual QA."
            }
          ],
          "issues": [],
          "rework_items": [],
          "summary": "Approved. All must criteria pass. The should criterion on line count is close enough given the component scope.",
          "task_verdicts": [
            {
              "task_id": "T6",
              "reviewer_verdict": "Pass. Claimed handler changes and command evidence match the repo state.",
              "evidence_files": ["megaplan/handlers.py", "megaplan/evaluation.py"]
            }
          ],
          "sense_check_verdicts": [
            {
              "sense_check_id": "SC6",
              "verdict": "Confirmed. The execute blocker only fires when both evidence arrays are empty."
            }
          ]
        }
        ```
        - `rework_items` must be an array of structured rework directives. When `review_verdict` is `needs_rework`, populate one entry per issue with:
          - `task_id`: which finalize task this issue relates to
          - `issue`: what is wrong
          - `expected`: what correct behavior looks like
          - `actual`: what was observed
          - `evidence_file` (optional): file path supporting the finding
          - `flag_id`: critique/review flag ID when applicable, otherwise `null`
          - `source`: short machine-readable source tag when applicable, otherwise `null`
        - `issues` must still be populated as a flat one-line-per-item summary derived from `rework_items` (for backward compatibility). When approved, both `issues` and `rework_items` should be empty arrays.
        - When the work needs another execute pass, keep the same shape and change only `review_verdict` to `needs_rework`; make `issues`, `rework_items`, `summary`, and task verdicts specific enough for the executor to act on directly.
